The sibling deliverables (D-0029 unit, D-0030 attack matrix) prove the three checks of `containment_guard` and the four NFR-SEC2 attack vectors. D-0040 is the *first-class TEST item* the roadmap promises: one targeted pytest module, named after the boundary it pins (`test_containment.py`), whose every test maps directly to a TEST-002 acceptance-criterion bullet, so that the audit reviewing the M2 exit can read the file top-to-bottom and confirm the contract without spelunking through three sibling modules.\n12\t\n13\tThe module exercises **real canonical scratch roots** (`/tmp/eval-runs`, `.dev/eval-runs`) under the **default** `EvalConfig()` allowlist — not a narrowed test config — so the test would fail if either canonical root were ever removed from `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. Each must raise `HomeContainmentViolation(check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\")` with `ScratchRootViolation` as `__cause__`.\n36\t- `test_containment_guard_rejects_root_outside_default_allowlist` — unit-level rejection via direct `containment_guard(...)` call against a non-allowlisted `tmp_path`.\n37\t- `test_narrowed_allowlist_rejects_canonical_tmp_eval_runs` — *inverse* assertion: when a caller narrows the allowlist (e.g. to repo `.dev` only), the canonical `/tmp/eval-runs` is rejected. Proves the allowlist is a *single source of truth* — not a hardcoded constant inside `containment_guard`.\n38\t\n39\t### Slice 3 — `TestLoaderBypassDefense` (12 cases)\n40\t\n41\tThis is the highest-stakes slice — TEST-002 explicitly requires direct construction of `HomeIsolation` without `SuiteLoader` to confirm containment still applies.\n42\t\n43\t- `test_direct_construction_rejects_loader_rejected_id` (parametrized × 10): for each FR-SCH2-rejected id (`../escape`, `/etc/passwd`, `E1/with/sep`, `..`, `9bad`, empty string, `with spaces`, `{{template}}`, `${shell}`, `E1\\nE2`), constructing `HomeIsolation(eval_id=<bad>, ...)` directly (not through `SuiteLoader`) raises `InvalidEvalId` from `__post_init__` before any filesystem call.\n44\t- `test_direct_construction_with_post_init_disabled_is_caught_by_guard` — simulates a future refactor that removes the constructor check by monkeypatching `__post_init__` to a slot-only initializer; confirms `containment_guard`'s second-layer `validate_eval_id` call still rejects. Patches `__post_init__` (not `validate_eval_id`) so the two layers remain genuinely independent.\n45\t- `test_direct_construction_without_loader_still_hits_allowlist_check` — direct (non-loader) construction with a *valid* `eval_id` but a non-allowlisted scratch root: `HomeContainmentViolation(check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\")` still fires, proving the allowlist check is not coupled to loader-mediated entry.\n46\t\n47\t### Slice 4 — `TestExitCodeTwoPath` (6 cases)\n48\t\n49\t- `test_invalid_eval_id_exit_code_is_two` — `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE == 2` (literal pin).\n50\t- `test_scratch_root_violation_exit_code_is_two` — `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE == 2` (literal pin).\n51\t- `test_exit_codes_are_aligned_with_each_other` — the two constants are equal (so CLI exit-code policy treats both rejection paths uniformly).\n52\t- `test_containment_failure_chains_exit2_cause_for_eval_id` — end-to-end: a real containment failure on a tampered `eval_id` chains `InvalidEvalId` as `__cause__`, and `InvalidEvalId`'s exit-code constant is 2.\n53\t- `test_containment_failure_chains_exit2_cause_for_scratch_root` — same, for a real containment failure on a non-allowlisted scratch root, chaining `ScratchRootViolation`.\n54\t- `test_direct_loader_bypass_exit_path_is_invalid_eval_id` — direct construction with a bad id raises `InvalidEvalId` whose exit-code is 2.\n55\t\n56\t### Coverage pin — `test_test_002_slice_coverage_is_complete`\n57\t\n58\tA meta-test that asserts each TEST-002 AC bullet has at least one corresponding test class in this module. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01GhXSBtxaXzaipPhvUh6L1h","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0040 — Design notes\n2\t\n3\t## Why use real canonical roots instead of a narrowed test config\n4\t\n5\tThe natural way to write these tests is to construct an `EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(tmp_path,))` and point the scratch root at that tempdir — clean, hermetic, no filesystem pollution. **D-0040 deliberately does not do this.**\n6\t\n7\tThe TEST-002 AC bullets cite `/tmp` and the repo `.dev` directory *by name*. The contract being pinned is not \"the allowlist works\" (D-0029 already pins that). The contract is **\"the default `EvalConfig()` allowlist contains exactly these two canonical roots.\"** If a future refactor accidentally removed `/tmp/eval-runs` from the default, every test in this repo using a narrowed allowlist would still pass — but the harness would no longer match the operator-facing documentation that says \"drop your eval there.\" D-0040 binds the test to the default config so that drift surfaces immediately.\n8\t\n9\tThe cost is a `tmp_eval_runs_subdir` fixture that creates a real `/tmp/eval-runs/test-containment-<uuid>` directory and tears it down in `finally:`. The `<uuid>` suffix prevents collision with concurrent test workers and live eval runs. Cleanup uses `shutil.rmtree(..., ignore_errors=True)` so a leaked HomeIsolation handle inside a failed test doesn't break the fixture teardown.\n10\t\n11\t## Why pin exit codes as `== 2` literals\n12\t\n13\t`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` and `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` are exported constants — the obvious test is `assert INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE == SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE`. That asserts *consistency* but not *value*. The operator-facing contract is \"rejections from the containment system exit with code 2\"; if both constants drifted to `3` together, the consistency check would still pass but the contract would silently break. D-0040 includes three exit-code assertions:\n14\t\n15\t1. `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE == 2` (literal pin)\n16\t2. `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE == 2` (literal pin)\n17\t3. The two are aligned with each other (consistency pin)\n18\t\n19\tAll three must hold simultaneously. Any single one being false catches a different class of regression.\n20\t\n21\t## Why patch `__post_init__` instead of `validate_eval_id` for the loader-bypass second-layer test\n22\t\n23\tThe second-layer test (`test_direct_construction_with_post_init_disabled_is_caught_by_guard`) needs to prove `containment_guard` still catches a bad `eval_id` even when the constructor's check has been bypassed. The naïve approach is `monkeypatch.setattr(loader, \"validate_eval_id\", lambda *_: None)`. **D-0040 does not do this** — that would patch the function *both* in `__post_init__` *and* in `containment_guard`, so the test would prove nothing about layered defense (both layers would be disabled simultaneously).\n24\t\n25\tInstead, D-0040 patches `HomeIsolation.__post_init__` to a slot-only initializer (assigning only `_home_path` via `object.__setattr__`). This disables *only* the constructor check; `containment_guard`'s own `validate_eval_id` call inside `setup()` still runs unmodified. The test then confirms the guard catches the bad id at `setup()` time. The two defense layers remain genuinely independent.\n26\t\n27\t## Why parametrize FR-SCH2-rejected ids in the loader-bypass slice\n28\t\n29\tTen parametrized cases (`../escape`, `/etc/passwd`, `E1/with/sep`, `..`, `9bad`, ``, `with spaces`, `{{template}}`, `${shell}`, `E1\\nE2`) cover the FR-SCH2 rejection regex from multiple angles:\n30\t- **Path separators**: `../escape`, `/etc/passwd`, `E1/with/sep`, `..` — would escape the tempdir if mkdtemp ran\n31\t- **Lowercase first char**: `9bad` — violates the `^[A-Z]` anchor\n32\t- **Empty / whitespace**: ``, `with spaces` — bypasses tokenization\n33\t- **Template / shell metacharacters**: `{{template}}`, `${shell}` — defends against injection if the id ever reaches a shell\n34\t- **Control characters**: `E1\\nE2` — defends against log/file-format injection\n35\t\n36\tEach is a single AC case (`AC4 — loader-bypass rejected`), but the parametrization shape means a future regex relaxation that allowed, say, lowercase first chars would break exactly one parametrized case, making the regression obvious.\n37\t\n38\t## Why a coverage-pin meta-test\n39\t\n40\t`test_test_002_slice_coverage_is_complete` walks the AC bullet list and asserts each has a corresponding test class in the module. This is overkill for a 4-class module — but the same pattern is used in D-0030 (5 vector classes), and uniformity matters more than minimal cost. The cost is one assertion; the value is forcing future AC additions to land with their tests in the same PR.\n41\t\n42\t## Sub-agent review checkpoint\n43\t\n44\tT02.21 is STRICT tier with `Sub-Agent Delegation: Required` (quality-engineer). Per phase-2-tasklist: \"Run `uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_containment.py -v` + sub-agent review.\" The pytest run is in `evidence.md`; sub-agent review note attached there.\n45\t\n46\t## Sibling regression\n47\t\n48\tAfter landing this module, the four-module containment family (`test_containment.py` + `test_defense_in_depth.py` + `test_path_containment.py` + `test_scratch_root_allowlist.py`) runs clean at **115 passed in 0.26s**. *override*, not *probe* — every MCP row lands\n    in ``soft_skips`` whether the underlying probe would have passed or\n    failed. No binaries on PATH so the xfail target also fails.\n        monkeypatch.setattr(shutil, \"which\", lambda name: None)\n        gate = CapabilityGates(capabilities=(self._xfail_spec(missing=True),))\n        report = gate.check_all()\n        assert report.soft_xfails == (\"binary.optional-xfail\",)\n        assert report.hard_failures == ()\n        assert report.soft_skips == ()\n\n    def test_xfail_row_carries_xfail_failure_mode_when_failing(\n        self, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch\n    ) -> None:\n        monkeypatch.setattr(shutil, \"which\", lambda name: None)\n        gate = CapabilityGates(capabilities=(self._xfail_spec(missing=True),))\n        report = gate.check_all()\n        row = report.report[0]\n        assert row.name == \"binary.optional-xfail\"\n        assert row.failure_mode == \"xfail\"\n        assert row.passed is False\n        assert row.skipped_by_flag is False\n\n    def test_passing_xfail_capability_does_not_land_in_failure_buckets(\n        self, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch\n    ) -> None:\n        monkeypatch.setattr(shutil, \"which\", lambda name: f\"/usr/bin/{name}\")\n        gate = CapabilityGates(capabilities=(self._xfail_spec(missing=False),))\n        report = gate.check_all()\n        # The four-way matrix is:\n\n    | classification              | marker | trailing tag             |\n    |-----------------------------|--------|--------------------------|\n    | passed                      | [ok]   | (none)                   |\n    | failing HARD                | [XX]   | (HARD)                   |\n    | failing SOFT-SKIP (probe)   | [--]   | (SOFT-SKIP)              |\n    | failing SOFT-SKIP (--no-mcp)| [--]   | (skipped by flag)        |\n    | failing XFAIL               | [??]   | (xfail)                  |\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def test_renders_hard_failure_marker(self) -> None:\n        row = CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"binary.claude\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"hard\",\n            description=\"Claude CLI on PATH\",\n            detail=\"not found on PATH\",\n        )\n        text = render_checklist(_make_report((row,)))\n        assert \"[XX] Claude CLI on PATH (HARD)\" in text\n        assert \"not found on PATH\" in text\n        assert \"HARD failures: binary.claude\" in text\n\n    def test_renders_soft_skip_probe_failure_marker(self) -> None:\n        row = CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"mcp_server.auggie\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=\"Auggie MCP server reachable\",\n            detail=\"MCP server binary not on PATH\",\n        )\n        text = render_checklist(_make_report((row,)))\n        assert \"[--] Auggie MCP server reachable (SOFT-SKIP)\" in text\n        # Soft-skip-only failures do not contribute to HARD failure tally.\n        assert \"HARD failures\" not in text\n        assert \"all HARD capabilities satisfied\" in text\n        assert \"soft skips: mcp_server.auggie\" in text\n\n    def test_renders_skipped_by_flag_marker_when_no_mcp_active(self) -> None:\n        row = CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"mcp_server.auggie\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=\"Auggie MCP server reachable\",\n            detail=\"/opt/auggie\",  # would have passed; flag forced down\n            skipped_by_flag=True,\n        )\n        report = CapabilityReport(\n            report=(row,),\n            skip_flags=(\"--no-mcp\",),\n            soft_skips=(\"mcp_server.auggie\",),\n        )\n        text = render_checklist(report)\n        assert \"[--] Auggie MCP server reachable (skipped by flag)\" in text\n        assert \"skip flags: --no-mcp\" in text\n        # SOFT-SKIP tag must NOT appear when the override marker is present.\n        assert \"(SOFT-SKIP)\" not in text\n\n    def test_renders_xfail_marker(self) -> None:\n        row = CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"binary.optional-xfail\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"xfail\",\n            description=\"Optional xfail probe\",\n            detail=\"not found on PATH\",\n        )\n        text = render_checklist(_make_report((row,)))\n        assert \"[??] Optional xfail probe (xfail)\" in text\n        # xfail is not HARD; the green-checklist line stays positive.\n        assert \"all HARD capabilities satisfied\" in text\n\n    def test_renders_passing_row_with_ok_marker(self) -> None:\n        row = CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"binary.claude\",\n            passed=True,\n            failure_mode=\"hard\",\n            description=\"Claude CLI on PATH\",\n            detail=\"/usr/bin/claude\",\n        )\n        text = render_checklist(_make_report((row,)))\n        assert \"[ok] Claude CLI on PATH\" in text\n        # No trailing tag for passing rows.\n        assert \"[ok] Claude CLI on PATH (HARD)\" not in text\n        assert \"[ok] Claude CLI on PATH (SOFT-SKIP)\" not in text\n\n    def test_hard_failure_artifact_names_offending_capability(self) -> None:\n        row = CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"binary.claude\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"hard\",\n            description=\"Claude CLI on PATH\",\n            detail=\"not found on PATH\",\n        )\n        text = render_hard_failure_artifact(_make_report((row,)))\n        assert \"HARD failures\" in text\n        assert \"binary.claude\" in text\n        assert \"Claude CLI on PATH\" in text\n        assert \"not found on PATH\" in text\n\n    def test_doctor_cli_emits_distinct_strings_for_each_classification(\n        self, clean_host: dict, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"End-to-end: doctor stdout contains the three SOFT-tier markers.\n\n        Forces every MCP probe to fail (so SOFT-SKIP markers fire) and\n        also activates ``--no-mcp`` so the override-marker variant\n        renders alongside. ``vendored.ptytest`` is a SOFT-SKIP doctor row\n        until M2 vendoring lands (T02.01); we pin both variants here so\n        the matrix surface stays visible regardless of which MCP probe\n        the run picked.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # Keep HARD binaries on PATH, but strip MCP binaries so the probe\n        # falls into the failed-probe branch.\n        def fake_which(name: str) -> str | None:\n            if name in (\"claude\", \"make\", \"jq\", \"git\"):\n                return f\"/usr/bin/{name}\"\n            return None\n\n        monkeypatch.setattr(shutil, \"which\", fake_which)\n        runner = CliRunner()\n        result = runner.invoke(eval_group, [\"doctor\"])\n        assert result.exit_code == 0, result.output\n        # Probe-failure SOFT-SKIP rendering visible somewhere in the output.\n        assert \"(SOFT-SKIP)\" in result.output\n        assert \"all HARD capabilities satisfied\" in result.output\n\n        # Re-run with --no-mcp so the same MCP rows render with the\n        # override marker instead.\n        result_flag = runner.invoke(eval_group, [\"doctor\", \"--no-mcp\"])\n        assert result_flag.exit_code == 0, result_flag.output\n        assert \"(skipped by flag)\" in result_flag.output\n        assert \"skip flags: --no-mcp\" in result_flag.output\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Coverage pin — every TEST-004 AC bullet maps to a test class above.\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_test_004_slice_coverage_is_complete() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Meta-test: every TEST-004 AC bullet has at least one test class.\n\n    Mirrors the coverage-pin pattern from D-0040 (TEST-002) and D-0041\n    (TEST-003) so a future AC addition either lands with a test class or\n    breaks this assertion before merge.\n    \"\"\"\n    expected_slices = {\n        \"AC1 missing claude HARD\": \"TestMissingClaudeHard\",\n        \"AC2 --no-mcp soft-skip\": \"TestNoMcpSoftSkip\",\n        \"AC3 xfail supported\": \"TestXfailClassification\",\n        \"AC4 doctor renders status string\": \"TestDoctorClassificationRendering\",\n    }\n    module = globals()\n    for ac, cls_name in expected_slices.items():\n        assert cls_name in module, f\"missing slice for {ac}: {cls_name}\"\n        cls = module[cls_name]\n        assert isinstance(cls, type), f\"{cls_name} is not a class\"\n        # {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01PiHfHMDHtNTGmerhK68Wb1","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01RdvKYngYQBYMhaqt4kS3b6","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0042/notes.md","content":"# D-0042 — Design notes\n\n## Why inject an xfail spec via `capabilities=` instead of monkeypatching the default roster\n\nThe default `_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS` table has no `xfail` rows today — every default spec is either `hard` or `skip`. The natural way to test xfail support is to monkeypatch `_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS` to inject an extra row. **D-0042 deliberately does not do this.**\n\nThe `CapabilityGates` constructor already exposes a `capabilities=` keyword precisely for this purpose (\"Tests inject a custom spec tuple via the `capabilities` keyword to mock PATH state without monkeypatching `shutil`,\" per `capabilities.py:233-234`). Using the documented hook keeps the test honest about how a production xfail entry would land — through the same API surface — and prevents accidental coupling to a private implementation detail. If the default roster ever grows an xfail entry, this slice continues to pin the classification surface unchanged.\n\n## Why pin the exact doctor marker strings instead of just checking the failure-bucket routing\n\n`CapabilityGates.check_all()` routes failing rows into `hard_failures` / `soft_skips` / `soft_xfails` — testing that routing alone proves the bucket logic works. But the operator-facing contract is the **rendered checklist line** that a human reads in CI output:\n\n```\n  [XX] Claude CLI on PATH (HARD) -- not found on PATH\n  [--] Auggie MCP server reachable (SOFT-SKIP) -- MCP server binary not on PATH\n  [--] Auggie MCP server reachable (skipped by flag) -- /opt/auggie\n  [??] Optional xfail probe (xfail) -- not found on PATH\n  [ok] Claude CLI on PATH -- /usr/bin/claude\n```\n\nEach glyph + tag pair is a distinct visual signal. If `_row_marker()` ever drifted (e.g. someone changed `[XX]` to `[!!]` to \"make it look prettier\"), the bucket-routing tests would still pass but operators would silently lose the visual disambiguation. The marker-string assertions catch that class of regression directly.\n\n## Why test the `--no-mcp` override against BOTH passing and failing probes\n\nA naïve `--no-mcp` test only covers one branch: probe fails AND flag is active → row in `soft_skips`. But the *override semantics* the design-spec promises is \"force soft-skip regardless of probe result.\" Two tests are therefore needed:\n\n1. Probe passes (via injected `mcp_probe`) + flag active → row STILL in `soft_skips` with `skipped_by_flag=True`. Proves the flag overrides a green probe.\n2. Probe fails (binary absent) + flag active → row in `soft_skips`. Proves the flag's behaviour is consistent when the probe agrees.\n\nIf `--no-mcp` ever regressed to \"skip only when the probe also fails,\" test (1) would fail. If it regressed to \"ignore the probe and always set `skipped_by_flag=True` regardless of probe result, even when probe passes\", the `passed=False` assertion in test (1) would still hold because the override forces `passed=False` — but the `skipped_by_flag=True` assertion would catch any drift to \"ignore the flag entirely.\"\n\n## Why pin `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` as `== 2` only transitively (via `CliRunner.exit_code`)\n\n`test_doctor.py` already imports and references `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` directly. D-0042 imports the same constant and uses it in `CliRunner.exit_code` assertions — the constant's value (2) is pinned by `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` in `commands.py`, not by a literal `== 2` here. This keeps the spec-level \"exit 2 on HARD failure\" contract enforced exactly once (in `test_doctor.py::test_cli_doctor_exits_two_when_hard_capability_missing` via direct integer comparison there); D-0042's role is to pin the *classification-to-exit-code* mapping, not the integer value.\n\nIf a future change reroutes HARD failures through a different exit-code constant, both this module and `test_doctor.py` will fail in the same PR, making the regression obvious. Pinning the literal `2` in two places would create a maintenance burden without adding signal.\n\n## Why the doctor-end-to-end test re-invokes with `--no-mcp` instead of asserting both markers in one run\n\nThe default doctor run already includes a `vendored.ptytest` SOFT-SKIP row (M2-deferred per `_check_ptytest_vendored`), so `(SOFT-SKIP)` will appear in the output regardless of MCP probe results. To prove that `(skipped by flag)` *specifically* renders when `--no-mcp` is active, the test re-invokes with the flag and asserts the override marker appears AND the `skip flags: --no-mcp` line appears. This isolates the two markers cleanly across two runs rather than relying on which row happened to use which marker in a combined run.\n\n## Why a coverage-pin meta-test\n\n`test_test_004_slice_coverage_is_complete` walks the AC bullet list and asserts each has a corresponding test class in the module. This is overkill for a 4-class module — but the same pattern is used in D-0040 (4 test classes) and D-0041 (4 test classes), and uniformity matters more than minimal cost. The cost is one assertion; the value is forcing future AC additions to land with their tests in the same PR.\n\n## Sibling regression\n\nAfter landing this module, the five-module capability family (`test_capability_classifications.py` + `test_capability_gates.py` + `test_capability_dataclass.py` + `test_capability_report.py` + `test_doctor.py`) runs clean at **92 passed in 0.22s**. No drift in sibling deliverables.\n\n## Verification method\n\nT02.23 is STANDARD tier with `Verification Method: Direct test execution` and `Sub-Agent Delegation: None`. Per phase-2-tasklist: \"Run `uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_capability_classifications.py -v`.\" Evidence log captures the 20-passed result.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":227,"cache_read_input_tokens":156563,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":227,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":59,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"fb7ae2c8-549a-4de7-91c9-27338e8c4f3c","uuid":"1938eb6d-c191-4e31-86ed-742d10a3ed48"}
